# 1.0 INTRODUCTION

## 1.1 SCOPE

**1.1.1** This Standard covers Shell Canada Limited (Shell) requirements for piping, valves and fittings for use in refineries, chemical plants and related facility piping systems, whose design is in accordance with ANSI B31.3, Chemical Plant and Petroleum Refinery Piping.

**1.1.2** This Standard is intended to establish a standard of materials and to provide a basic guide for piping designers and engineers.

**1.1.3** The introductory Sections of this Standard (1.0 through 5.0 inclusive) must be attached to the selected Piping and Tubing Classes for the specific project.

**1.1.4** Any deviation from this Standard requires prior written approval by Shell Engineering Services Division.

## 1.3 CONSTRUCTION CLASSES

**service requirements**

**1.3.1** Piping systems are specified by the designer as one of three quality levels called **Construction Classes**. The appropriate class is chosen by the designer based on severity of service as per Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design. Construction Class 1 is the highest quality, whereas Construction Class 3 is the lowest quality.

**quality**

**1.3.2** Each Piping Class has one or more permitted Construction Classes tabulated in Section 4.0, Piping Class Listing, based on the construction quality that will result from using the specified piping components, and the extent of inspection and construction procedures. The extent of inspection and construction procedures are described in design, manufacturing and installation standards.

**matching quality to service**

**1.3.3** Piping Classes chosen for an application must be permitted for the design construction class specified by the designer. Use of a Piping Class with a permitted construction class rating that falls below the design Construction Class will result in inadequate quality for the intended service. Use of a Piping Class with a permitted Construction Class that exceeds the design Construction Class will result in excessive quality for the intended service. If no appropriate Piping Class exists, a new one must be generated and approved by Shell Engineering Services Division.

## 2.0 PIPING CLASS ABBREVIATIONS

### 2.1 GENERAL

**2.1.1** Piping Classes are identified by a three-letter code system, (e.g., SAB).

The first letter designates SERVICE, the second letter designates MATERIAL and the third letter designates RATING (ANSI Class).

**NOTES:**

(1.) For glycol/water heat tracing Piping Class refer to Shell STD. 3-3.32, Glycol Tracing - Installation, Class GAA.

(2.) Hydrogen service is defined as process streams containing relatively pure hydrogen or component streams containing hydrogen above a partial pressure of 50 psia at design conditions.

(3.) Process service is defined as the handling of liquid and/or gaseous hydrocarbon materials and also includes lube oil, nitrogen, DEA, sulphur dioxide, carbon dioxide, phenol, ammonia, hydrogen sulphide, glycol other than tracing and boiler feed water.

## 2.5 SUFFIXES

Piping Class suffixes shall be used in the following cases:

- o **(H)** This denotes either a change in the bolting and/or gasket specification due to higher operating temperature of the piping. Refer to sub-sections 3.4 and 3.5.
- o **(SR)** This denotes piping is to be post-weld heat treated (stress relieved). This suffix is used for Carbon Steel only.
- o **(T)** This denotes Tubing Classes utilizing tubing and associated fittings. Tubing Classes are referenced to corresponding Piping Classes. Refer to Section 5.0, Tubing Class Listing.
- o **(1)** This denotes decrease in corrosion allowance from the minimum standard. Refer to sub-section 3.9.
- o **(2)** This denotes increase in corrosion allowance from the minimum standard. Refer to sub-section 3.9.

## 3.0 GENERAL NOTES

**3.1** PIPE

All pipe shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 3-2.01, Piping Components - Manufacturing. All alloy pipe shall be subject to Shell STD. 18-2.01, Testing and NDE Methods and Procedures for Pressure Equipment and Piping, Appendix E.

**3.2** FITTINGS

**3.2.1** All fittings shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 3-2.01, Piping Components - Manufacturing. All alloy fittings shall be subject to Shell STD. 18-2.01, Testing and NDE Methods and Procedures for Pressure Equipment and Piping, Appendix E.

**3.2.2** The descriptions of fittings such as thredolet, sockolet, weldolet, etc. are generic only.

**3.2.3** "Bull Plugs" shall be of the solid, round head, threaded type.

**3.2.4** For special fittings such as strainers, refer to Shell STD. 3-2.01, Piping Components - Manufacturing. All special fittings of alloy material shall be subject to Shell STD. 18-2.01, Testing and NDE Methods and Procedures for Pressure Equipment and Piping, Appendix E.

**3.3** FLANGES

All flanges shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 3-2.01, Piping Components - Manufacturing. All alloy flanges shall be subject to Shell STD. 18-2.01, Testing and NDE Methods and Procedures for Pressure Equipment and Piping, Appendix E.

**3.4** BOLTING

All bolting shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 3-2.01, Piping Components - Manufacturing.

**important** NOTE that bolting in the Classes is specified by temperature. Many Classes show two bolting specifications for one Class. The user shall create a new Class with a suffix (H) to avoid the substitution of lower temperature limit materials [e.g. - PCE(H)], where (H) indicates change in the bolting specification for the higher temperature].

## 3.5 GASKETS

**3.5.1** All gaskets shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 3-2.01, Piping Components - Manufacturing.

**important** **NOTE** that gaskets are specified in the Piping Classes by temperature or Construction Class. Some Piping Classes show two gasket types. The user shall create a new Piping Class with a suffix (H) to avoid the substitution of lower temperature limit materials or less severe Construction Class [e.g., PCEIH; where (H) indicates change in gasket specification for the higher temperature].

**3.5.2** Compressed asbestos composition gaskets are allowed for Construction Class 3, unless otherwise specified in Piping Classes.

**3.5.3** Spiral-wound gaskets with asbestos filler may be used where allowed by local regulations. They shall be limited to service temperatures up to 455 C (850 F). Flexible graphite filler may be used for service temperatures up to 705 C (1300 F).

**3.5.4** Piping Classes with ANSI Class 1500 flanges at service temperatures over 455 C (850 F) may utilize welded joint gasket design where justified by piping stress analysis, in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

## 3.6 TUBING

**3.6.1** All tubing for steam tracing shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 3-3.31, Steam Tracing - Installation, Classes T1 and T2.

**3.6.2** Tubing for glycol/water tracing shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 3-3.32, Glycol Tracing - Installation, Class GAA.

**3.6.3** Tubing for instrumentation shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 16-1.12, Process Measurement Devices - Design.

**3.6.4** Tubing for piping installations shall be purchased in accordance with this Standard.

## 3.7 VALVES

**3.7.1** Gate, globe and check valves shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 3-2.01, Piping Components - Manufacturing.

**3.7.2** Ball valves shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 3-2.01, Piping Components – Manufacturing.

**3.7.3** High-performance butterfly valves shall be purchased in accordance with Shell STD. 3-2.01, Piping Components – Manufacturing.

**stem packing**

**3.7.4** Valve stem packing shall be asbestos or alternative material approved by Shell Engineering Services Division, for temperature range -29 C (-20 F) to 455 C (850 F), in the following services: hydrocarbon fluids, hydrogen, hydrogen sulphide mixture, saturated steam, superheated steam, condensate, oil, water, liquid sulphur, corrosive hydrocarbon, solvents and other general refinery applications **excluding** strong acids and alkaline substances. Refer to Shell STD. 3-2.01, Piping Components – Manufacturing for acceptable manufacturers/designs of stem packing.

For service temperatures up to 705 C (1300 F), use die-formed, flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings).

**3.7.5** When specifying use of "soft-seated" valves, refer to the manufacturer's recommended pressure-temperature rating.

**3.7.6** Manually operated gear drives shall be provided as follows, unless high pressure drop considerations dictate otherwise:

**Gate**

ANSI Class 150    10 in. & larger
ANSI Class 300     8 in. & larger
ANSI Classes 600 & 900    6 in. & larger
ANSI Class 1500    4 in. & larger

**Globe** – All Classes    Same as Gate

**Ball & Butterfly**

Ball – All Classes    6 in. & larger
Butterfly – All Classes    8 in. & larger

**3.7.7** Pressure instrument gauge valve installation shall be in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design. For pressure-temperature ratings other than shown in the Piping Classes consult the manufacturer.

**3.7.8** Cast steel valves quality shall be in accordance with Shell STD. 6-2.01, Pressure Retaining Castings - Valves and Fittings - Manufacturing.

**3.7.9** All alloy valves shall be subject to Shell STD. 18-2.01, Testing and NDE Methods and Procedures for Pressure Equipment and Piping, Appendix E.

## 3.8 VALVE TRIM

**3.8.1** The valve trims to be used are indicated on the individual Piping Classes.

**3.8.2** In hydrogen service, "Free-machining" trim materials shall not be used (e.g., Type 416 SS in Carbon Steel Classes or Type 303 SS in Stainless Steel Classes).

**3.8.3** In "wet" H₂S service, only "soft" trim meeting the requirements of NACE MR0175, latest edition, is acceptable (e.g., Stellite over Carbon Steel or ASTM Type 316 SS).

**3.8.4** The user of this Standard shall verify with the valve supplier/manufacturer that the type of trim provided is suitable for the intended service.

## 3.9 CORROSION ALLOWANCE

**3.9.1** Standard minimum corrosion allowance is 1.6 mm (1/16 in.) to cover most applications in hydrocarbon service and is included in the pipe schedules in the individual Piping Classes.

**important** **NOTE** that smaller pipe sizes normally have minimum corrosion allowance for economic reasons. Larger pipe sizes normally have 3.2 mm (1/8 in.) of wall thickness available for corrosion allowance.

**3.9.2** Different corrosion allowance may be required depending on service conditions. Upon advice of the Shell Materials/Corrosion Engineer, the designer may increase or decrease the corrosion allowance.

**3.9.3** If additional corrosion allowance is required, a separate Piping Class shall be created with a suffix (2) to denote increase in the corrosion allowance [e.g., PAB2)]. A separate Piping Class shall be created with a suffix (1) to denote decrease in the corrosion allowance [e.g., PJB(1)].

**3.9.4** When the Piping Class indicates that wall thickness calculations are required and a minimum pipe schedule is specified, then the selected wall thickness shall not be less than the one specified in the Piping Class.

## 3.10 WALL THICKNESS

**3.10.1** The minimum wall thickness shall be in accordance with ANSI B31.3 Code rules.

**3.10.2** Piping for which wall thickness has been calculated for pressure only shall have its thickness not less than the minimum allowable wall thickness as shown on Table 3.1. Also, see sub-section 3.10.3.

**3.10.3** The **minimum allowable wall thickness** shown in Table 3.1 may be used as the basis for calculation of the required wall thickness for carbon steel components, only within the following pressure, temperature and pipe size limits:

> **Table - Pressure \(max.\), Temperature \(max.\) and Pipe \(NPS\) size limits** - see [XLSX](tables/03-piping-materials-and-line-classes-01_p0014_tbl01_pressure-temperature-pipe-size-limits.xlsx) and [source crop](tables/03-piping-materials-and-line-classes-01_p0014_tbl01_pressure-temperature-pipe-size-limits.png)

| Pressure (max.) | | Temperature (max.) | | Pipe |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| kPa | (PSI) | C | (F) | (NPS) |
| 2400 | (350) | 260 | (500) | 14 and below |
| 1380 | (200) | 260 | (500) | 16 to 24 |

The **required wall thickness** used to select the pipe schedule shall be the **minimum allowable wall thickness** (from Table 3.1, where applicable) plus the corrosion allowance (from the Piping Class).

![Required Wall Thickness = Min. Allowable Wall Thickness + Corrosion Allowance](figures/03-piping-materials-and-line-classes-01_p0014_fig01_required-wall-thickness-equation.png)

**3.10.4** For piping operating outside of the limits in sub-section 3.10.3, or where full stress analysis was done, the minimum wall thickness shall be based on calculations. Calculations shall take into account **all loads** identified in ANSI B31.3.

> **Table - Table 3.1 Minimum Allowable Wall Thickness for Carbon Steel Piping \(See Sub-Section 3.10.3\)** - see [XLSX](tables/03-piping-materials-and-line-classes-01_p0015_tbl01_table-3-1-min-allowable-wall-thickness-c.xlsx) and [source crop](tables/03-piping-materials-and-line-classes-01_p0015_tbl01_table-3-1-min-allowable-wall-thickness-c.png)

**TABLE 3.1    MINIMUM ALLOWABLE WALL THICKNESS .FOR CARBON STEEL PIPING (SEE SUB-SECTION 3.10.3)**

| PIPE (NPS) | MIN. ALLOW. WALL THICKNESS |
| :---: | :---: |
| 3/4 to 3 | 2.54 mm (0.10 in.) |
| 4 to 6 | 3.05 mm (0.12 in.) |
| 8 to 24 | 3.30 mm (0.13 in.) |

**NOTE:** The above minimum allowable wall thickness limits shall not be used as arbitrary retirement limits. For inspection purposes, arbitrary retirement limits are indicated in Shell EG 118-52.01, General Plant Inspection Guidelines – Shutdown Planning and Inspection.

## 3.11 IMPACT TESTING

The requirements for impact testing are to be based on the application of materials and in accordance with ANSI B31.3 Impact Testing Methods and Acceptance Criteria for Metals and as specified in Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.

## 3.12 HEAT TREATMENT

**3.12.1** Pipe, valves and fittings shall be heat treated as specified in the individual Piping Classes.

**3.12.2** When PWHT is required for Carbon Steel Piping Classes, the user shall create a new class with a suffix (SR) added to the line Class designation (e.g., SAGS8R).

## 3.13 MANUFACTURING

**3.13.1** Fabrication and quality control of shop fabricated piping, shall be in accordance with Shell STD. 3-2.02, Piping – Shop Fabrication.

**3.13.2** Fabrication and quality control of field fabricated piping, shall be in accordance with Shell STD. 3-3.01, Piping – Field Fabrication and Installation.

**3.13.3** Underground process and utility piping operating up to 60 C (140 F), shall be coated and wrapped in accordance with Shell STD. 19-2.11, External Shop Coating of Pipe for Underground Service – Manufacturing or Shell STD. 19-3.11, External Field Coating for Underground Pipe – Installation.

**3.13.4** Piping Classes which specify pipe and fittings in 2 NPS and under shall be socket-welded. When service considerations justify, buttwelded construction in 2 and 1-1/2 NPS is permissible and shall be in accordance with the requirements of Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design, Shell STD. 3-2.02, Piping – Shop Fabrication and Shell STD. 3-3.01, Piping – Field Fabrication and Installation.

## 3.14 OPERATING AND INSTRUMENT CONNECTIONS

**3.14.1** Operating and instrument connection details shall be in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Piping – Design.

**3.14.2** Operating connections are vents, drains, hydrotest connections, etc. Instrument connections are thermowells, pressure instruments, instrument bridles, etc.

**3.15 TERMINAL CONNECTIONS AND VALVED BRANCH CONNECTIONS**

**3.15.1** The term "Nipples" in this Standard means short pieces of pipe 1-1/2 NPS and smaller. Nipples shall be between 50 mm (2 in.) minimum and 150 mm (6 in.) maximum in length and have a wall thickness as indicated in the notes of the respective Piping Classes.

Nipples shall be used only between piping or equipment, and an adjacent block valve for terminal connections and valved branch connections.

Short pieces of pipe 1-1/2 NPS and smaller, used between various piping components are not considered nipples and shall have the same wall thickness as the schedule of a pipe run or arrangement.

**3.15.2** A terminal connection is defined as a minimum length of pipe 1-1/2 NPS and smaller, connected to other piping components or equipment that terminates at a plugged valve (e.g., vents, drains, etc.).

**3.15.3** A valved branch connection is defined as a minimum length of pipe 1-1/2 NPS and smaller, connected to other piping components or equipment, that includes but does not terminate at an adjacent block valve (e.g., sampling connections, drain lines, vent lines, etc.).

**3.16 DESIGN OF BRANCH CONNECTIONS**

For application of equal or reducing tees, weldolets, sockolets and stub-ins for branch connections, refer to Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.

**3.17 HYDROGEN SERVICE**

The actual upper temperature limit for the application of Piping Classes in hydrogen services listed in this Standard, must not exceed limitations of "Nelson" curves published in the latest edition of API Publication 941.

**3.18 HYDROGEN SULPHIDE SERVICE**

All materials in hydrogen sulphide service shall be in accordance with NACE MR0175.

## 3.19 OPERATING BLINDS

Operating blanks (or blinds) shall conform with API Standard 590, Steel Line Blanks and the following:

- Blank materials shall be equivalent to those specified in the respective Piping Class.
- Gasket surfaces shall be in accordance with that specified for adjacent flanges in the respective Piping Class.
- The corrosion allowance shall be increased where deemed necessary by the designer.
- Handles and webs shall be integral with blanks or spacers.

## 4.0 PIPING CLASS LISTING

## NOTES:

( 1.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 2.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 250-500 microinch (6.4 to 12.5 micrometres) AARH.

( 3.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Components shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.

( 4.) Connection between hydrant and main header to be a carbon steel fitting.

( 5.) The type and material of inlet will depend on location. Those locations that have slight frost penetration use a straight section (cast iron) adaptor between header and hydrant. Those locations that have heavy frost penetration use an elbow (carbon steel) between header and hydrant. Confirm design with location.

( 6.) Sizes and types of hose thread and cap nut to be confirmed by location.

( 7.) Hydrant to be either self draining or with drain depending on location.

( 8.) Where attached to buttweld fittings, use weld neck flanges.

( 9.) Use flat-face flanges against cast iron flanges.

(10.) All below-ground pipe shall be externally coated as per Shell STD. 19-2.11, External Shop Coating of Pipe for Underground Service - Manufacturing or Shell STD. 19-3.11, External Field Coating of Underground Pipe - Installation.

(11.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body socket/weld only, is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify the normalized condition.

(12.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

(13.) Refer to Table 5.1 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

1. Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
2. All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 250-500 microinch (6.4 to 12.5 micrometres) AARH.
3. Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Components shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated conditions.
4. Connection between hydrant and main header to be a carbon steel fitting.
5. The type and material of inlet will depend on location. Those locations that have slight frost penetration use a straight section (cast iron) adapter between header and hydrant. Those locations that have heavy frost penetration use an elbow (carbon steel) between header and hydrant. Confirm design with location.
6. Sizes and types of hose thread and cap nut to be confirmed by location.
7. Hydrant to be either self draining or with drain depending on location.
8. Where attached to buttweld fittings, use weld neck flanges.
9. Use flat-face flanges against cast iron flanges.
10. All below-ground pipe shall be externally coated as per Shell STD. 19-2.11, External Shop Coating of Pipe for Underground Service - Manufacturing or Shell STD. 19-3.11, External Field Coating of Underground Pipe - Installation.
11. Cl018 CS valve, normalized body socket weld only, is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify the normalized condition.
12. Acceptable valves are: HEX HG661, Century CM9 or AGCO M5K. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD, 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
13. Refer to Table 5.1 for the Tubing Class.

**NOTES:**

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings. Above 260 C (550 F), use A-106 GR.B SMLS.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to ANSI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.

( 5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.

( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 250-500 microinch (6.4 to 12.5 micrometres) AARH.

( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.

( 8.) Purchase Order shall identify that valves will be installed in steam service.

( 9.) Combination Trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.

(10.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.

(11.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

(12.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

(13.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.

(14.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

(15.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(16.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd'd valve to API 602.

(17.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify the normalized condition.

(18.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

(19.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings. Above 260 C (500 F), use A-106 GR.B SMLS.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.

( 5.) Use flanges.

( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.

( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.

( 8.) Purchase Order shall identify that valves will be installed in steam service.

( 9.) Combination Trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.

(10.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.

(11.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

(12.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

(13.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.

(14.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

(15.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(16.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd'd valve to API 602.

(17.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.

(18.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

(19.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

| **ITEM** | **SIZE** | **RATING** | **TYPE** | **SPECIFICATIONS** | **NOTES** |

**NOTES:**

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings. Above 260 C (500 F), use A-106 GR.B SMLS.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.

( 5.) Use flanges.-

( 6.) If flanges used, serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.-

( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.-

( 8.) Purchaser Order shall identify that valves will be installed in steam service.-

( 9.) Combination Trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.-

(10.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.-

(11.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

(12.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

(13.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.

(14.) For wall thickness (including C.A.) greater than 3/4" user is to consult with Shell Engineering Services Division regarding heat treatment. See sub-section 3.12.

(15.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched 160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

(16.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(17.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd valve to API 602.

(18.) When PWHT is required, use valves with bolted bonnets and covers. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.

(19.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.

(20.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO M5K. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1-01, Plant Piping - Design.

(21.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends or rolled threads are acceptable.
( 5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.
( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 250-500 microinch (6.4 to 12.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 8.) Combination/Trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
( 9.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.
( 10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 12.) Where valves with non-metallic seats, seals, liners, etc. are used, the manufacturer's pressure and temperature ratings shall limit the service of this Class.
( 13.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.
( 14.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched 160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
( 15.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 16.) These valves shall only be used up to a max. operating temperature of 150 C (300 F).
( 17.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd valve to API 602.
( 18.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body socket/weld only, is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
( 19.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.
( 20.) Refer to Table 5.1 for the Tubing Class.
( 21.) Refer to Table 5.1 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Swages made of bar/pipe conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.
( 5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.
( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 8.) Combination Trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
( 9.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.
(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(12.) Where valves with non-metallic seats, seals, liners, etc. are used, the manufacturer's pressure and temperature ratings shall limit the service of this Class.
(13.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify the heat-treated condition.
(14.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched 160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(15.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(16.) These valves shall only be used up to a maximum operating temperature of 150 C (300 F).
(17.) See sub-section 3.5.
(18.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd'd valve to API 602.
(19.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
(20.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(21.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Swages made of bar-stock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.
( 5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.
( 6.) All applications of this Class must be reviewed by Shell's Materials/Corrosion Engineer.
( 7.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 8.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 9.) Combination Trim 316 SS with Stellite seats is acceptable.
(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(12.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.
(13.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched 160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(14.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(15.) PWHT is required at minimum 620 C (1150 F). Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(16.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
(17.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(18.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

(1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings. Above 260 C (500 F), use A-106 GR.B SMLS.

(2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

(3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

(4.) Swages made of full stock conforming to ANSI C-1025 fully-killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.

(5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under. See Note (17).

(6.) The pressure rating of Class 150 flange is less than 150 psi at less than 340 C (650 F).

(7.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.

(8.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.

(9.) Combination Trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.

(10.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.

(11.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

(12.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

(13.) In benzene service, flanges shall be kept to a minimum and serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.

(14.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.

(15.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched 160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

(16.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(17.) In benzine service do not use unions.

(18.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thr'd valve to API 602.

(19.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.

(20.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.

(21.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.

( 5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.

( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.

( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.

( 8.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.

( 9.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.

(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 190 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

(12.) Where valves with non-metallic seats, seals, liners, etc. are used, the manufacturer's pressure and temperature ratings shall limit the service of this Class.

(13.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.

(14.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

(15.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(16.) These valves shall only be used up to a maximum operating temperature of 150 C (300 F).

(17.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd valve to API 602.

(18.) CI101 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.

(19.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A loop body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.

(20.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Swages made of forgings, conforming to ANSI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.

( 5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.

( 6.) All applications of this Class must be reviewed by Shell Materials/Corrosion Engineer.

( 7.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.

( 8.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.

( 9.) Combination trim 316 SS with Stellite seats is acceptable.

(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

(12.) Heat-treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.

(13.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched 160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

(14.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(15.) PWHT is required at minimum 620 C (1150 F). Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.

(16.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.

(17.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG464, Century CM9 or AGCO M5K. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

(18.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings. Above 260 C (500 F) use A-106 GR.B SMLS.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.
( 5.) Use flanges.
( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 8.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
( 9.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.
( 10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 12.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.
( 13.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
( 14.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 15.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thr'd valve to API 602.
( 16.) CI018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
( 17.) Actuators: valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures/temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings. Above 260 C (500 F) use A-106 GR.B SMLS.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.

( 5.) Use flanges.

( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.

( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.

( 8.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.

( 9.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.

(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

(12.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify the heat treated condition.

(13.) For wall thickness (including C.A.) greater than 3/4" user is to consult with Shell Engineering Services Division regarding heat treatment. See sub-section 3.12.

(14.) Use 600# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

(15.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(16.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thr'd valve to API 602.

(17.) When PWHT is required, use valves with bolted bonnets and covers. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.

(18.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.

(19.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.

(20.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.XXS Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.
( 5.) Use flanges.
( 6.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 7.) All flange sensations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 8.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service. See sub-section 3.14.
( 9.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 1 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(12.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify the heat-treated condition.
(13.) Flexible wedge (not including CA) greater than 3/4" user is to consult with Shell Engineering Services Division regarding heat treatment. See sub-section 3.12.
(14.) When PWHT is required, valve bodies, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(15.) C1018 CS valve, normalized condition is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
(16.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.
(17.) Refer to Table 5.4 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Use flanges.

( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.

( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.

( 7.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.

( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

( 10.) Use 600# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

( 11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

( 12.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.

( 13.) Valve stem packing shall be die-formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455 C (850 F).

( 14.) See sub-section 3.4.

( 15.) See sub-section 3.5.

( 16.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.

( 17.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

( 18.) Refer to Table 5.3 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 microntres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service. See sub-section 3.14.
( 7.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 10.) Use 600# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
( 11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 12.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.
( 13.) Valve stem packing shall be die-formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455 C (850 F).
( 14.) See sub-section 3.5.
( 15.) See sub-section 3.5.
( 16.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
( 17.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
( 18.) Refer to Table 5.2 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Use flanges.

( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.

( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service. See sub-section 3.14.

( 7.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.

( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

(10.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

(11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(12.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.

(13.) Valve stem packing shall be die-formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455 C (850 F).

(14.) See sub-section 3.14.

(15.) See sub-section 3.5.

(16.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.

(17.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressure-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

(18.) Refer to Table 5.2 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipple and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.XXS Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service. See sub-section 3.1.4.
( 7.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(10.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(11.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.
(12.) Valve stem packing shall be die-formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455 C (850 F).
(13.) See sub-section 3.4.
(14.) See sub-section 3.5.
(15.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(16.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(17.) Refer to Table 5.2 for the Tubing Class.

( 1.1) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 10.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
( 11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 12.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered conditions. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.
( 13.) Valve stem packing shall be die-formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 45 C (850 F).
( 14.) See sub-section 3.4.
( 15.) See sub-section 3.5.
( 16.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
( 17.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long extension body may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.
( 18.) Refer to Table 5.3 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(10.) Use 600# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(12.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered conditions. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.
(13.) Valve stem packing shall be die-formed flexible graphic rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455 C (850 F).
(14.) See sub-section 3.4.
(15.) See sub-section 3.5.
(16.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(17.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1-01, Plant Piping - Design.
(18.) Refer to Table 5.3 for the Tubing Class.

- (10.) Use 600# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

| **ITEM** | **SIZE** | **RATING** | **TYPE** | **SPECIFICATIONS** | **NOTES** |

**NOTES:**

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched XXS Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Combination 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable. See sub-section 3.1.4.
( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(10.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(11.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.
(12.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455 C (850 F).
(13.) See sub-section 3.4.
(14.) See sub-section 3.5.
(15.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(16.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(17.) Refer to Table 5.2 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Normally, use unions for 1 NPS and under.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 9.) Where valves with non-metallic seats, seals, liners, etc. are used, the manufacturer's pressure and temperature ratings shall limit the service of this Class.
(10.) Unions and SO flanges are limited to 260 C (500 F) and to non-cyclic services.
(11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(12.) See sub-section 3.4.
(13.) 304 SS/HFS trim is acceptable.
(14.) Where SW valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd valve.
(15.) The pressure rating of Class 150 flange is less than 150 psi at less than 340 C (650 F).
(16.) Where attached to buttweld fittings, use weld neck flanges.
(17.) Acceptable valves are: HEX 1G461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required when the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(18.) Refer to Table 5.3 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service. See sub-section 3.14.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 9.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(10.) See sub-section 3.4.
(11.) 304 SS/HFS trim is acceptable.
(12.) Where SW valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd'd valve.
(13.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO M5K. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(14.) Refer to Table 5.2 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) For 1 NPS and under normally use unions.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 9.) Where valves with non-metallic seats, seals, liners, etc. are used, the manufacturer's pressure and temperature ratings shall limit the service of this Class.
(10.) Unions and SO flanges are limited to 260 C (500 F) and in non-cyclic services.
(11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(12.) See sub-section 3.4.
(13.) 316 SS/HFS trim is acceptable.
(14.) Where SW valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thrd valve.
(15.) The pressure rating of Class 150 flange is less than 150 psi at less than 340 C (650 F).
(16.) Where attached to buttweld fittings, use weld neck flanges.
(17.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(18.) Refer to Table 5.3 for the Tubing Class.

**NOTES:**

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 9.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(10.) See sub-section 3.4.
(11.) 316 SS/HFS trim is acceptable.
(12.) Where SW valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thrd valve.
(13.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(14.) Refer to Table 5.3 for the Tubing Class.

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service. See sub-section 3.14.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 9.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 10.) See sub-section 3.4.
( 11.) 316 SS/HFS trim is acceptable.
( 12.) Where SW valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd'd valve.
( 13.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO M5K. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
( 14.) Refer to Table 5.2 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Combination Trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
( 8.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.
( 9.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(10.) Flexible swage is acceptable.
(11.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched 160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(12.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(13.) Heat treated means in normalized or normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify the heat-treated condition.
(14.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd'd valve to API 602.
(15.) When PWHT is required, use valves with bolted bonnets and covers. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(16.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
(17.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(18.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

(1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.

(2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

(3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

(4.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.

(5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.

(6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.

(7.) Combination Trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.

(8.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.

(9.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

(10.) Flexible wedge is acceptable.

(11.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched 160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

(12.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(13.) Heat treated means in normalized or normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify the heat-treated condition.

(14.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd'd valve.

(15.) When PWHT is required, use valves with bolted bonnets and covers. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.

(16.) Cl018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.

(17.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

(18.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

**NOTES:**

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.
( 5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.
( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 8.) Combination Trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
( 9.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.
(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable.
(12.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.
(13.) When PWHT is required use Class CAB(SR). For details refer to Metals Section - Corrosion Data Survey, NACE - Caustic Soda Chart.
(14.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.
(15.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(16.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thr'd valve to API 602.
(17.) CI018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
(18.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(19.) Refer to Table 5.6 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Swages made of barstock, conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.

( 5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.

( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.

( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.

( 8.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

( 9.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections only.

(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable.

(12.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.

(13.) PWHT is required. For details refer to Metals Section - Corrosion Data Survey, NACE - Caustic Soda Chart. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.

(14.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.

(15.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

(16.) Refer to Table 5.6 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Swages made of bar-stock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.
( 5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.
( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 8.) Combination Trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
( 9.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.
(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable.
(12.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.
(13.) When PWHT is required, use Class CAD(SR). For details refer to Metals Section - Corrosion Data Survey. NACE - Caustic Soda Chart.
(14.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(15.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(16.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thr'd valve to API 602.
(17.) CI01S CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
(18.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(19.) Refer to Table 5.6 for the Tubing Class.

| **SERVICE:** | Caustic: Concentration 20% by Weight and Less, Between 70 C (160 F) and 98 C (210 F), Concentrati |
| **BASIC MATERIAL:** | Carbon Steel (13) |
| **CONSTRUCTION CLASS:** | 2 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; **CORROSION ALLOWANCE:** 1.6 mm (0.063") |

*(Refer to ANSI B16.5 - Latest Edition for Pressure-Temperature Limits)*

**CAD(SR)**

| **ITEM** | **SIZE** | **RATING** | **TYPE** | **SPECIFICATIONS** | **NOTES** |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.
( 5.) Normally, use unions for 1-1/2 NPS and under.
( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 8.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
( 9.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable.
(12.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.
(13.) PWHT is required. For details, refer to Metals Section - Corrosion Data Survey, NACE - Caustic Soda Chart. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(14.) C1018 CS valve normalized body is acceptable. Valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
(15.) An acceptable valve is HEX-461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.
(16.) Refer to Table 5.6 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.
( 5.) Use flanges.
( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 8.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable. Only Type 410 SS is permitted.
( 9.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.
(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(12.) Refer to API 941 ("Nelson" curves) for temperature limit for material at applicable H₂ -∂- P.P. The lower temperature limit of either Nelson curves or 205 C (400 F) shall apply.
(13.) Where valves with non-metallic seats, seals, liners, etc. are used; the manufacturer's pressure and temperature ratings shall limit the service of this Class.
(14.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.
(15.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(16.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(17.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thrd valve to API 602.
(18.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
(19.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.
(20.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.
