## NOTES:

(1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
(2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
(3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
(4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with plain ends are acceptable.
(5.) Use flanges.
(6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
(7.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
(8.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable. Only Type 410 SS is permitted.
(9.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.
(10.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(11.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(12.) Refer to API 941 ("Nelson" curves) for temperature limit for material at applicable H - 2• P.P. The lower temperature of either Nelson curves or 205 C (400 F) shall apply.
(13.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.
(14.) For wall thickness (including C.A.) greater than 3/4" user is to consult with Shell Engineering Services Division regarding heat treatment. See sub-section 3.12.
(15.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(16.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(17.) Where API 606 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd valve to API 602.
(18.) When PWHT is required, use valves with bolted bonnets and covers. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(19.) C1019 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
(20.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG661, Century CM9 or AGCO M5K. A long body extension may be required where line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(21.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched XXS Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to ANSI C-1025 fully killed and stress relieved with rolled ends are acceptable.

( 5.) Use flanges.

( 6.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.

( 7.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service. See sub-section 3.14.

( 8.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable. Only Type 410 SS is permitted.

( 9.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

(10.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 1 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

(11.) Refer to API 941 ("Nelson" curves) for temperature limit for material at applicable H- -> P.P. The lower temperature limit of either Nelson curves or 205 C (400 F) shall apply.

(12.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.

(13.) For wall thickness (including C.A.) greater than 3/4" user is to consult with Shell Engineering Services Division regarding heat treatment. See sub-section 3.12.

(14.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(15.) When PWHT is required, valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.

(16.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.

(17.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM0 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

(18.) Refer to Table 5.4 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

(1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
(2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
(3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
(4.) Use flanges.
(5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
(6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
(7.) Combination trim 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable. Only Type 410 SS is permitted.
(8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(10.) Refer to API 941 ('Nelson' curves) for temperature limit for material at applicable H+ → P.P.
(11.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(12.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(13.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify the heat-treated condition.
(14.) See sub-section 3.4.
(15.) See sub-section 3.5.
(16.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455 C (850 F).
(17.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(18.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(19.) Refer to Table 5.3 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched XXS Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service. See sub-section 3.14.
( 7.) Combination 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable. Only Type 410 SS is permitted.
( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150°C (300°F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 10.) Refer to API 941 ("Nelson" curves) for temperature limit of material at applicable H• •2• P.P.
( 11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 12.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to __ identify heat treated condition.
( 13.) See sub-section 3.4.
( 14.) See sub-section 4.3.
( 15.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455°C (850°F).
( 16.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
( 17.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
( 18.) Refer to Table 5.2 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Combination 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable. Only Type 410 SS is permitted.
( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(10.) Refer to API 941 ("Nelson" Curves) for temperature limit of material at applicable H+ - 2+ P.P.
(11.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(12.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(13.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.
(14.) See sub-section 3.4.
(15.) See sub-section 3.5.
(16.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (ball rings) above 455 C (850 F).
(17.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(18.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(19.) Refer to Table 5.3 for the Tubing Class.

(11.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(12.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(13.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.
(14.) See sub-section 3.4.
(15.) See sub-section 3.5.
(16.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455 C (850 F).
(17.) PWHIT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(18.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(19.) Refer to Table 5.2 for the Tubing Class.

**NOTES:**

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched XXS Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service. See sub-section 3.14.
( 7.) Combination 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable. Only Type 410 SS is permitted.
( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 10.) Refer to API 941 ("Nelson" Curves) for temperature limit of material at applicable H+ –2+ P.P.
( 11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 12.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.
( 13.) See sub-section 3.4.
( 14.) See sub-section 3.5.
( 15.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455 C (850 F).
( 16.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
( 17.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
( 18.) Refer to Table 5.2 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched.160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 4.) Use flanges.

( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.

( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.

( 7.) Combination 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable. Only Type 410 SS is permitted.

( 8.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

( 9.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.

(10.) Refer to API 941 ('Nelson' Curves) for temperature limit of material at applicable H+ - 2+ P.P.

(11.) Use 6000# rating when branches are Sched.160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.

(12.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

(13.) Heat treated means in normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.

(14.) See sub-section 3.4.

(15.) See sub-section 3.5.

(16.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings) above 455 C (850 F).

(17.) PWHT is required. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.

(18.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

(19.) Refer to Table 5.3 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 9.) "Free machining" trim is not permitted.

(11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(12.) 304 SS/HBS trim is acceptable.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 9.) "Free machining" trim is not permitted.
(10.) See sub-section 3.4.
(11.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(12.) 304 SS/HFS trim is acceptable.
(13.) Where SW valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd'd valve.
(14.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO M5K. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping – Design.
(15.) Refer to Table 3.3 for the Tubing Class.

( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched. 80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 9.) "Free machining" trim is not permitted.
( 10.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 11.) See sub-section 3.4.
( 12.) 316 SS/HF5 trim is acceptable.
( 13.) Where SW valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd valve.
( 14.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO M5K. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures/temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping, Design.
( 15.) Refer to Table 3.3 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 9.) "Free Machining" trim is not permitted.
( 10.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 11.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings).
( 12.) Where SW valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd'valve.
( 13.) See sub-section 3.4.
( 14.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO M5K. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Use flanges.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
( 9.) "Free Machining" trim is not permitted.
(10.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(11.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings).
(12.) Where SW valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thr'd valve.
(13.) See sub-section 3.4.
(14.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO M5K. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures/temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(15.) Refer to Table 3.3 for the Tubing Class.

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings.
( 2.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 3.) Use flanges.
( 4.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 80S Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 5.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.
( 6.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 7.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
( 8.) The ratings of SW flanges shall be reduced by one-third of the values shown in ANSI B16.5.
( 9.) Heat treated means in the normalized and tempered condition. Components shall be stamped to identify heat-treated condition.
( 10.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
( 11.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings).
( 12.) See sub-section 3.4.
( 13.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1-01, Plant Piping – Design.
( 14.) Refer to Table 5.7 for the Tubing Class.
( 15.) For pressure-temperature ratings not listed in ANSI B16.5 tables, establish these ratings in accordance with Annex D in B16.5 (1981).

( 1.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.

( 2.) Use flanges.

( 3.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125–175 microinch (3.2 to 4.5 micrometres) AARH.

( 4.) Use 3/4 NPS and 2 NPS for vent and drain service. See sub-section 3.14.

( 5.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.

( 6.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 80 Min. See sub-section 3.15.

( 7.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.

( 8.) Valve stem packing shall be die formed flexible graphite rings with braided graphite filament end rings (bull rings). See sub-section 3.4.

( 9.) See sub-section 3.4.

(10.) An acceptable valve is HEX HG461. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1-01, Plant Piping - Design.

(11.) Refer to Table 5.2 for the Tubing Class.

(12.) For pressure-temperature ratings not listed in ANSI B-16.5 tables, establish these ratings in accordance with Annex D in B16.5 (1981).

## NOTES:

( 1.) Service conditions may require heavier wall pipe and fittings. Above 260 C (500 F), use A-106 GR.B SMLS.
( 2.) Nipples and swages 1-1/2 NPS and under shall be Sched 160 Min. See sub-section 3.15.
( 3.) Schedule of fittings and WN flanges to be the same as pipe schedule.
( 4.) Swages made of barstock conforming to AISI C-1025 fully killed and stressed relieved with plain ends are acceptable.
( 5.) Use flanges.
( 6.) Purchase Order shall identify the valves that will be installed in steam service.
( 7.) All flange serrations shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.
( 8.) Use 3/4 NPS for vent and drain service.
( 9.) Combination 13 CR with Stellite seats is acceptable.
(10.) Bolted bonnet may be considered as an alternative for 2 NPS.
(11.) Use flanged valves on nozzles and flanged equipment or where space is limited.
(12.) Flexible wedge is acceptable. Gate valves 3 NPS and larger in service above 150 C (300 F) shall have flexible wedge.
(13.) Use 600# rating when branches are Sched 160 or heavier. See sub-section 3.15.3.
(14.) Heat treated means in normalized condition. Hardness shall not exceed 187 HB. Forgings shall be stamped (N) to identify heat-treated condition.
(15.) Use 2 NPS for flanged operating and instrument connections. See sub-section 3.14.
(16.) Where API 600 valve cannot be used, substitute SW/female thd'd valve to API 602.
(17.) When PWHT is required, use valves with bolted bonnets and covers. Valve bonnets, covers, gaskets, internals and trim shall be removed and tagged prior to stress relief. Reassemble with new gaskets.
(18.) C1018 CS valve, normalized body is acceptable. The valve body shall be stamped (N) to identify normalized condition.
(19.) Acceptable valves are: HEX HG461, Century CM9 or AGCO MSK. A long body extension may be required where the line is insulated. A gauge valve may be used only for design pressures-temperatures within its rating and only for pressure instruments installations in accordance with Shell STD. 3-1.01, Plant Piping - Design.
(20.) Refer to Table 5.5 for the Tubing Class.

**NOTES:**

( 1.) Use bolting insulation kits when connecting aluminum flanges to carbon steel or stainless steel flanges. Not required for aluminum-to-aluminum flanges.

( 2.) Pipe supports shall be clamp-on carbon steel with teflon insulation between pipe and clamp.

( 3.) Aluminum piping and flanges shall be fireproofed with insulation and 304 SS sheeting. Insulation shall be warranted by the manufacturer as suitable for direct application on aluminum and shall not contain corrosive or other harmful components.

( 4.) Flange gasket face shall be to ANSI B16.5, 125-250 microinch (3.2 to 6.4 micrometres) AARH.

( 5.) For socket weld connections, bore out thread. Valves shall be dismantled prior to welding of pipe to valve body.

( 6.) For vents and drains use 3/4 NPS valves with one end threaded for round head solid aluminum "bull plug".

( 7.) ANSI B16.5 Class 150 dimensions.
